1.  General Information

  1.1 Field Introduction

Azadegan South Oil Field is situated in West Karoun province on a large long-axis anticline with N-S
trending with approximate dimensions about 20 km width and 60 km length near to Iran-Iraq border, to
the east of Yaran Oil Field.

The trap area of the south Azadegan is limited to 43.2x17.2 km. The Azadegan Oil field was discovered
in 1999, when Well AZN-1 was drilled vertically down to the Cretaceous Fahliyan Formation with total
depth of 4,643 m. The results of this well, revealed the reservoir characteristics of the field. Following to
the result of the well no. AZN-1, another 5 exploration wells was drilled in different parts of this huge
field down to Fahliyan Formation. In the later stage the well No.10 assumed as exploration well for
evaluation of deeper formations. This well was drilled in 2008, down to the depth of 5340m (Neyriz
Fm.), mainly to evaluate the Jurassic formations.

Based on the exploration wells results, carbonate reservoir of Sarvak Formation is broadly developed in
Azadegan Field. This formation was penetrated in all of seven exploration wells (AZN-1, AZN-2, AZN-
3, AZN-4, AZN-5, AZN-6 and AZN-10). Clastic oil-bearing zones of Kazhdumi Formation developed in
main anticline of Azadegan Field, extending from north to center of South Azadegan. The Clastic oil-
bearing zones of Gadvan Formation developed only in crest area and south parts of South Azadegan
Filed, which was discovered by AZN-1 in crest and AZN-4 in south parts. The carbonates of Fahliyan
Formation also developed in Azadegan field, but the oil reservoir of this formation developed in South
part of South Azadegan.

The Azadegan Field is an elongated anticline, trending in N-S direction, belonging to "Arabian Trend".

The stratigraphy sequence of the field includes AghaJari, Gachsaran, Asmari, Fahliyan, Pabdeh, Gurpi,
Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak, Kazhdumi, Dariyan, Gadvan and Fahliyan Formations, from top to bottom. This
sequence in well no AZN-10, the deepest up to now drilled well of the field, continues with Garau,
Gotnia, Najmah, Sargalu, Alan, Mus and Neyriz formations.

The Dominant lithofacies of Sarvak Formation are Rudist-bearing packstone and partially grainstone,
which contains abundant bioclasts. Some other facies like matrix-dominated wackestone and mudstone
are also found.

The AZADEGAN Oilfield is located in Khuzestan province and is approximately located 80 Kilometers
West of Ahwaz City, near to Iran-Iraq Border. It is in the Northwest of the YADAVARAN Field and in
the West of JUFEYR  Field. the average monthly Temperature  is 45ºc  in summer, and highest

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
           South Azadegan Oilfield Project

   Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                   Page 7 of 181

temperature is 52ºc. The lowest temperature is -4.4ºc in winter. Maximum rainfall is 78 mm in February,
winter Season.

  1.3 F-20 Well Location Map

F-20, which is Fahliyan producer as a vertical well, is planned to be drilled in pad-93, located amongst
AZNS-070-ST2 & AZNS-049 wells as Fahliyan wells & AZNS-173 as Gadvan well in the area-III. This
well was supposed to be drilled in pad-72, then a decision was made by client to change the pad to a new
location (pad-93), so the geoname was changed from G20 to F20.

  1.4 Reservoir Information

 1.4.1 Key Reservoir Descriptions
Fahliyan Formation: This carbonate to argillaceous carbonate Formation is limited by overlying thick
argillaceous successions in the upper part (eaquivalent to the Iraqi Ratawi Formation) and the main part
consists of different bioclastic limestones with some layers of shale and argillaceous facies that can be
assumed equivalent with Iraqi Yamama Formation. However, in South Azadegan wells 1, 2 and 5 the
Fahliyan Formation is characterized by fossiliferous limestone beds and interbedded shales. It should be
noted that most of Majnoon wells penetrated into the Sarvak and Fahliyan carbonates as one of the main
reservoir targets.

 1.5.1 Expected Stratigraphy and Formation Top in F-20
The area is completely covered by the alluvium of Holocene age; the drilled will be Aghajari, Gachsaran,
Asmari, Pabdeh, Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak, Kazhdumi, Dariyan, Gadvan and Fahliyan formations. A
brief stratigraphic sequence has been predicted as follows:

     -    Aghajari Formation (surface to 1021mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 1021 m)
The Aghajari Formation consists of a repetition of brown to grey calcareous sandstones, chert debris and gypsum-veined red marl. A fauna

of brackish to freshwater was observed, together with more marine fauna.. Aghajari overlays Gachsaran Formation with a conformable

boundary.

     -   Gachsaran Formation (1021-1368 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 347m)
The Gachsaran Formation with a thickness ranges from 400 m to 500 m thickens towards the north. Deposition of the Gachsaran Formation

is cyclic. The cycle began with a deposition of shallow-water marine carbonates, and then it was overlain by a thick evaporitic phase. The

evaporates were capped by red to variegated marl, which represented the end of cycle. Gachsaran is laid on Asmari Formation with a

comformable boundary.

     -   Asmari Formation (1368-1583 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 215 m)
The Asmari Formation in Azadegan Field developed a massive sandstone interval, with a few thin argillaceous limestones in the upper

sections. Sandstone are clear, fine to medium-sorted, and poorly consolidated. On the other hand, the lower sections consist of

intercalation of sandy, chalky, slightly dolomitic high-energy limestone with sandstone layers. Asmari is parallel to Pabdeh Formation at the

bottom.

     -   Pabdeh Formation (1994-2178 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 165 m)
Pabdeh Formation is divided into two parts in the Azadegan field. The upper part is made of grey to dark-brown argillaceous limestones and

marls. Planktonic fauna dates that this part was deposited in Oligocene. The lower part is composed of dark-brown, slightly argillaceous

limestone. Pelagic fauna, mostly Globigerina and Globorotalia, indicates that this part was formed during late Paleocene to late Eocene in

age. It seems that no early Paleocene Formation exists in the Azadegan area. Chert and cherty limestone layers at the bottom of Pabdeh

Formation are very important for drilling parameters on the PDC bit (as a notification for drilling operation department).

     -   Gurpi Formation (2178-2560mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 382 m)
Gurpi Formation consists of argillaceous limestone with higher clay content rather than Pabdeh Formation. About 50 meters of pure

limestone will be observed in the middle part of the formation which divides it into 3 different parts as below:

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
           South Azadegan Oilfield Project

   Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 15 of 181

     -   Upper Gurpi (2178-2343mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 165 m)
The Gurpi Formation in the Azadegan Field consists of grey to dark-brownish, slightly argillaceous micritic limestone with Maestrichtian to

Campanian aged fauna. At Well AZN-01, about 50 meters of chalky limestones were encountered in the middle of the Gurpi formation,

which contains abundant fragments of rudists, bryozoa, corals, and Orbitoides.

     -   Tarbur Member (2343-2418 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 75 m)
In the western Abadan Plain about 50 meters thick chalky limestone was encountered in the middle part of the Gurpi Formation, which

contains abundant fragments of bioclasts, suggesting very shallow depositional environment. This interval is equivalnt to the Tarbur

Formation with reservoir quality in other regions.

     -   Lower Gurpi (2418-2560 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 142 m)
This member consists of gray, light gray, medium hard argillaceous Limestone. The Gurpi formation is overlain directly on Ilam formation by

conformity contact.

     -    Ilam Formation (2560-2629 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 69 m)
In the Azadegan Field, the Ilam Formation consists of grey micritic limestone, often argillaceous, rarely pyritic, limestones. Microfauna,

mostly pelagic, including rotalids and oligostegina assemblage, a few algae, bryozoa, and rudist fragments exist.

     -    Laffan Member (2629-2635mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 6 m)
The Laffan Formation developed a thin greenish-grey pyritic shale layer with ostracods fauna that capping the Sarvak Formation.

     -   Sarvak Formation (2635-3270 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 635 m)
The Sarvak Formation developed a carbonate unit, which was gradually overlying the Kazhdumi Formation, and it is abruptly terminated by

an erosion phase, covered by a various late Cretaceous sediment. The lithology of Sarvak consists of the dark grey fine grained nodular

argillaceous limestone interbedded with thin marls in the bottom. The Limestone graded upward into a white-to-buff massive chalky

limestone with siliceous nodules; developed a very massive tan-to-brown limestone containing abundant rudist debris in the middle part. In

the upper part is made up of rubbly limestone with red staining and leached zones of breccia and the top of Sarvak Formation is very

ferruginous.

     -   Kazhdumi Formation (3270-3464 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 194 m)
Kazhdumi Formation begins with dark brown, soft to medium hard, some fissile and calcareous Shale. This formation is start with dark

brown to dark green gray, medium hard to hard, soft to medium, high bituminous, calcareous Shale interbedded with argillaceous

limestone, sandy claystone, claystone, marl, sandy limestone, limestone beds. The Argillaceous Limestone is dark brown to brown gray,

medium hard to hard, micro bituminous. The Sandy claystone is light green gray, soft, high calcareous. The Claystone is gray to light gray,

soft. The Marl is green gray, soft, sticky. The Sandy Limestone is dark green gray, hard, faint sandy. The Limestone is light gray to green

gray, medium hard to soft, partly argillaceous. The Kazhdumi Formation at base is consisting of Azadegan Member.

     -   Dariyan Formation (3464-3622 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 158 m)
In Dariyan Formation a variety of lithological rock types such as carbonates, rich organic shales and sandstones were deposited in various

environments. The lower part of the Gadvan Formation is composed of low-energy, but not very deep, micritic, often argillaceous limestone

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
           South Azadegan Oilfield Project

   Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 16 of 181

with echinoids and algal fragments. Two sandstone units were defined in the Gadvan Formation lateral changes and especially discontinuity

of the sandstone layers which are consist of fine clastics (shales and siltstones) may be expected

The Gadvan formation divided to three part:Dariyan formation starts with white to white cream grey clean limestone type I/II, I. Base of

Dariyan formation, appears grey, green Pyritic shale interbedded with argillaceous limestone. This zone called transition zone and thickness

is 16m. after last grey limestone indicates of Gadvan formation.

This carbonate formation contains three zones. D1 and D3 are non-reservoirs and D2 is a moderately poor reservoir.

     -   Gadvan Formation (3622-3915 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 293 m)
In This Formation a variety of lithological rock types such as carbonates, rich organic shales and sandstones were deposited in various

environments. The lower part of the Gadvan Formation is composed of low-energy, but not very deep, micritic, often argillaceous limestone

with echinoids and algal fragments. Two sandstone units were defined in the Gadvan Formation lateral changes and especially discontinuity

of the sandstone layers which are consist of fine clastics (shales and siltstones) may be expected

The Gadvan Formation divided to three part;

Upper Gadvan; the upper part starts with occurrence of a terrigenous rock. The lithology is composed of grey claystone and some shale.

Khalij Mbr.; appears with pure limestone, cream, light brown and in some intervals oil stain.

Lower Gadvan; consisting of alternating of marl, argillaceous limestone, limestone, claystone, sandy claystone and sandstone beds. The

Marl is green gray, soft, sticky, slightly calcareous. The Argillaceous Limestone is gray white gray medium hard to soft, partly pyritic. The

Limestone is white, white to light gray, hard. The Claystone is light gray, soft, sticky. The Sandy Claystone is light gray, soft, sticky, fine

sand grained. The Sandstone is brown to gray, partly white buff, gray, friable, sorted, sub rounded, medium quartz grained, partly

calcareous. This sandstone which occur in Zone-3 and Zone-5 are the reservoir of this formation and in this part is equivalent with Kushk

member.

     -    Fahliyan Formation (3915-4180.0 mMD)(TD) (Drilled Thickness: 265m +)
This formation is start with light gray, light green gray, soft Limestone interbedded with green gray, soft, sticky Marl, green gray, light gray,

soft, Argillaceous Limestone and dark green gray, soft, sticky Clay stone beds. This formation at continue is consisting of buff white light

gray, light brown, brown, medium hard, hard, densely, slightly argillaceous Limestone and off white, white gray, medium hard Chalky

Limestone                                                                                                                beds.

activities. In geological operation, the required mud logging activites are as below:

    Depth controlling

    Lag depth calculation through mud Pump GPM and drilling depth

    Gas samples gathering, analysis and giving the related reports

    Preparing the Well Geological Final Report according to the standard formats.
    Kill sheet must be submitted during drilling.

  1.8 General Assumption

 1.8.1 Well Objective, Target and Total Depth

      1.8.1.1  Purpose of the Well
This well will be completed as a fahliyan oil producer at vertical hole.

      1.8.1.2  Wellbore Quality Evaluation Criteria:

    1)  Maximum Deviation and Horizontal Displacement for Vertical Wells

                                                           Bottom Hole            Depth(m)                   Deviation (°)                              DLS（°/30m）                                                                                 Shift (m)
            ≤2000                   ≤3°                  ≤50                        0≤2.25°

           2000~3000                  ≤5°                  ≤80                         ≤2.50°

           3000~4000                  ≤7°                 ≤120                        ≤2.75°

           4000~5000                  ≤9°                 ≤160                       ≤3°

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
          South Azadegan Oilfield Project

Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 40 of 181

 2)  Other requirements for Wellbore Quality
   Average borehole diameter enlargement rate less than 15%
   All casing/liner are run to designed depth (normally 1-2m above TD/section TD)
  No fills at bottom of hole
  No junk lost in hole
  No abnormal casing wear and casing deformation caused by CONTRACTOR’s malpractice
 3)   Well trajectory control key points
    Drill upper formation before KOP using Pendulum BHA, low WOB and high RPM, to ensure vertical hole drilled.
   Control the distance between two holes in one well pad is not less than 2 meters. Single-shot and multi-shot will be
    used to monitor the well trajectory. Take single shot survey at interval of 100m; run multi-shot survey each 300m
    from surface to kick off point. If necessary, survey shall be run more frequently.
  Run build up BHA (Motor + MWD) whenever collision risk increases to build up a little inclination (2~4°) and
    bottom hole departure to avoid collision at vertical section if necessary.
    If magnetic interference exists, gyroscopic inclinometer shall be used in order to avoid collision.
  Amend the planned profile according to the data obtained by electronic multi-shot or single-shot survey in vertical
     hole, and locate the kick off point.
  MWD/GR will be used in directional drilling to ensure that the actual well path will coincide with the planned well trajectory to the
     extent that it shall not pass through the boundaries of the corridor that surrounds the planned hole trajectory in case a corridor has
    been specified.
  LWD/GR will be utilized in horizontal drilling in order to controlling well trajectory, especially in Sarvak-3.
   The downhole motor and MWD shall be used in vertical hole section if necessary.
   Monitors and analyses the build-up ability of the BHA used in directional/horizontal section. Due to the lagged survey data
    measured by MWD, inclination, azimuth and the buildup rate should be predicted based on the instant survey at all times.  If
     necessary, more surveys should be taken so as to adjust drilling parameters and BHA, control the well trajectory within the
      specified target tolerance strictly.
    In buildup section, to avoid pipe-stuck, BHA should be designing such way to minimize sliding mode time especially in clay
     formations. To reduce torque in rotating mode and consequently reduce sliding mode time, using stabilizer with short gauge and
       profile.
   Using circulating sub above MWD is recommended due to sever loss in this area.
    Adjust the drilling method according to the well survey data, and control the change amplitude of inclination and azimuth to ensure
     the smoothness of the well trajectory.
    In this section, the torque and drag are relatively higher, torque and drag analysis for each BHA run should be performed, and
     torque and drag reduction measures (for example simplifying the BHA or running cuttings bed impeller) are recommended.

3.  Sequence of Drilling Operation

 3.1 Rig Move

    Hold pre-move meeting on location. Discuss contingency plans for:
                       Safe arrangements for rig up and rig down.
                       Safe standby areas for rig
                       In case move have to be suspended.
                       Loss of trucks in case of repair.
                       Moving at night.
    Hold pre-move safety meeting. Ensure everyone understands individual responsibilities:
    Commence rigging down. The ordering of trucks will be done by rig manager.
    Commence rig move to F20, PAD NO. 93

                       Position rig for optimal operations
    Rig up on cellar
                       Rig up and commence operational procedure for (F20)
                       Before leaving previous location, all equipment should be checked.
    Test all manifold and surface safety valves
                       RKB – GLE and Elevation
                       Spud date and time.
    Mud tanks should be empty during move. After moving Fill water tanks and keep as full as possible.

 3.2 Pre Spud

 3.2.1 Certificate

    All rig equipment is on location, properly rigged up and function-tested. Check availability and content of hoisting
       equipment inspection & maintenance records according to API.
    All rig equipment including all Tubulars, Subs, Tools & … should be inspected & according to API.

 3.2.2 Safety Device

    Test draw-works crown block saver and record results on daily drilling report; test derrick man escape device
       (Geronimo line) and record time required for test weight on daily drilling report; test rig intercom system, especially
         for Driller-Derrick Man and Driller-Mud Logger communication link.
    Check that all rig safety equipment, e.g., fire extinguishers, eye-wash stations etc., is installed at the proper locations
        as per rig layout diagrams.
    Verify that substructure is level and foundation adequate to support drilling unit during whole well period and that
       mast is centered above rotary table.
    Ensure rig alignment with Top Drive System. Make sure rig tongs and rig floor hoisting equipment are adequate for
        handling 8-1/2″ and 9-1/2″ drill collars. Make sure that mechanical tong torque gauges for drill pipe/drill collar are
        properly functioning and have been calibrated; re-calibrated as required.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
          South Azadegan Oilfield Project

  Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 49 of 181

3.2.3 Rig Equipment

   Verify that the 26″ tri-cone bit is ready to be picked up, all nozzles installed, and 26″ bit breakers are on the rig floor.
     Make sure single-shot or multi-shot survey tools available for vertical section survey.
   Make sure all solids control equipment are correctly installed and have been function-tested with charging pressure
      gauges in place where applicable. Make sure adequate screen inventory for the shale shakers is available in the sizes
       required for the well. Make sure water tanks are completely filled and transfer lines from water tanks to mud system
       are hooked up. Make sure all mud materials and chemicals required to drill 26″ section is on location, in sound
       condition and suitably stored. Make sure that bentonite is pre-hydrated and sufficient volume has been built for top
       section and for well-kill.
   Make sure that all mud pumps have been fitted with new consumable parts: liners, pistons, valves, seats etc. Make
       sure that pulsation dampeners are charged with Nitrogen to the proper pre-charge pressure, 30% of expected
       standpipe pressure during drilling of the 26″ hole section; check if sufficient Nitrogen bottles are available on
       location to adjust pulsation dampener pre-charge pressure as required. Make sure that mud pump pop-off valves are
        set at the proper pressure for the liner sizes installed and that the mud discharge lines from the pop-off valves to the
     mud tanks are properly secured. Check that all high-pressure hoses are properly secured with safety chains. P/T
       should be done to high-pressure surface line of circulating system.

3.2.4 Wellhead & BOP

   Have 2″ line pipe nipple with 2″ ball valve welded to 30″ conductor pipe 0.2m above cellar bottom to drain cement
      from conductor/riser pipe after cementing 20″ surface casing. Verify that pre-installed 30″ conductor pipe is vertical
      and that no angle/bend exists between conductor and 30″ riser pipe.
   Make sure that adequate supply of 20″ casing is on location, inspected and ready to be run, float shoe should already
      be made up, centralizers should be pre-installed on the pipe-rack prior to spud in the well.
   Ensure mud logging unit is functioning and equipment are calibrated and tested properly.

3.2.5 Others
   Verify that the rig’s fuel tanks are full to meet minimum of three days rig consumption available on site.
   Make sure all waste treatment equipment available and commissioning test is ok.
   Hold pre-spud meeting with relevant service Company personnel on location. Discuss procedures involved
       throughout 26″ hole section drilling, reporting system and critical safety operation measures.
  OEOC prepared a pre-spud checklist and distributed it in rigs. The checklist will be performed based on the
      aforementioned checklist. In addition to the checklist, following matters should be checked:

   Check & observe the alignment of the rig. This check will be performed in four stages; rig levelling, derrick
        verticality, derrick alignment with rotary table, rotary table alignment with conductor pipe. To do this pick up 3 joints
       of 9 3/4" DC and check the verticality of the derrick.
   Mechanical facilities for mud treatment such as:

               1. Desander, Desilter, (Degasser, mud centrifuge), mud cleaner, proper size of shaker–screens, hoppers, mud
            guns & mixers, should be all available on rig and functioning properly.
               2. All mud tanks, mud transfer lines & connection should be checked by filling them with water & cross
             wellhead circulation.
               3. Mud engineer laboratory should be equipped with a complete set of testing equipment. The calibration
             should be done under supervision of well site drilling supervisor before starting the operation.
               4. Weight indicator, rotary speed gauge, any smart drilling instrumentation, Top Drive, Stroke meter, pressure
            gauges on manifold and in the drilling console, loss / gain gauge and any other gauges should be checked and
               calibrated.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
           South Azadegan Oilfield Project

   Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 50 of 181

                 5. (Grand manifold valves & related lines), Stand pipe manifold (lines) valves & connection must be checked
        & pressure tested.
                 6. All motors, (SCR & VFD) should be checked under load prior to start drilling.
                 7. Check & control mud stock & cement stock every day & if there is any necessity to request mud material,
             cement or any drilling tool, it should be prepared & requested at proper time.
Note: Fill up following forms and send to drilling office:
    Pre-spud check list
    Location Handed Over

  3.3 Drilling

 3.3.1 26” Hole Section, 20” Casing

After commencing spud-in, 26” hole drilling will be started. The drilling would be anticipated to go on fast.

          Casing Point                 20” Casing @ 150 mMD

          Formation                      Aghajari

           Risks & Hole problems          Bit balled up

           Deviation control           INCLINOMETER (TOTCO)

          Logging                               ---

           Bit Type                      R. Bit (1-1-5)

                                         Pre-Hydrated Bentonite mud

                    MW          65-67
            Drilling Fluid
                            PV           5-18 CP

                                             Vis.         >50

                       WOB         10-30
            Drilling Parameters        GPM         900-1000
                          RPM       Max available

      3.3.1.1  Sequence of Operation
      Make up 26” BHA as per BHA design. Tag formation at ±14m.
        Drill ahead formation down to 150 m (20” casing point in Aghajari formation) using Hi-vis bentonite pill. (pump Hi-
        Vis Pill Periodically) Pay enough attention to drill straight hole.

Note:
In Case of complete loss while drilling 26” hole Cure by pump LCM pill or cement as cement plug.
      TD should be adjusted to have 1m of rat hole below casing shoe.
       The exact TD will be determined based on casing tally.
      Run Inclinometer (Totco) each 50m to evaluate the verticality of the hole.
       Ensure that all drilling tools and equipment are on well site and are in serviceable condition.
       Ensure that adequate fishing tools for this section of hole are available.
       Enough Mud Material, LCM and cement should be available on rig site to use in case of needing.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
           South Azadegan Oilfield Project

   Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 51 of 181

       At TD, Circulate and clean the hole. Spot high viscosity pill at bottom, drop Inclinometer (Totco) POOH to surface.
       Check Bit.
       Perform hole condition prior to run casing. If observe any indication of drag, run back to bottom. Wash and ream to
       make sure that the hole condition is good.
       Perform safety meeting.
      R/U casing running equipment.
      Run ±150m (Approx. 13 Jts) 20", 94 ppf, K-55, BTC casing with stab-in duplex shoe to bottom as per casing
        program. (function test of F.shoe should be checked on surface by WSS). Casing shoe should be adjusted to have 1m
         of rat hole and enough stick up (1 m on rig floor). Fill casing by non-LCM fresh mud.

     Note:
       Take care when running casing not to set down excessive weight if tight hole or ledges are encountered.
      Run stinger on DP’s using the “C” Plate & 5” x 20” centralizer. Sting in. M/U & test cementing lines. Circulate for
        one casing annulus volume. While circulating, check the DPs annulus for any return. Use all DPs weight on the string
            if observing any minor leakage through DPs annulus.
       Cement casing as per program. Any minor adjustment regarding the corrected depth will be done in due time.
        Displace with mud (1-2 bbls Short Displace). Monitor for pressures. Record and report the final pressure, Sting out.
       Check the float. POOH one stand, Circulate to flush/clean drill pipe.
       After cement returns to cellar floor, open the 2" nipple on conductor & flush it.
      POOH to surface. L/D stinger.
       Keep the casing on the elevator until cement surface samples get hard. Check for casing to be center inside conductor
         pipe.

Note:
       Based on the results obtained from cementing, decision will be made to perform top jobs.
        If there is no cement return to surface, a top job should be performed to fill the annulus. Use same slurry weight as
         the primary cement slurry. Ensure the availability of adequate X-over for connecting line pipe to cement line. The
        cement should be accelerated with max. (2%) Calcium Chloride BWOC and no other additives. Wait for the top job
        cement to get hard.
       Check casing by slack off weight.
       Cut conductor pipe. Rough cut landing joint and L/D C.P. and L.J. Dress and bevel 20” suitable to install and to weld
        casing head housing.
        Install and weld Casing Head Housing 20 ¾” – 3000 psi on casing and pressure test the sealing of welded casing.
         (Limit pressure to a maximum of 80% of the collapse strength of the casing, recommended testing pressure: 400 psi).
         Outlets must be aligning with cellar stairs.
       Before installing the casing head housing, open all the test ports and clean inside it with water and air.
       Check the 20 ¾” wear bushing, 20 ¾” Plug Tester and 13-3/8” Casing Slip and seal assembly in CHH before
          installing CHH (on Surface).
      N/U 21 ¼”-3000 BOP stack and related lines and valves and test all to 2400psi.
      N/U Bell Nipple & flow line
       P/U & M/U Next BHA and stand drill pipes to 2182m.
        Fill up the following forms and send to drilling office:

                Casing Tally
          BHA reports
                  Bit Reports
                Casing Report
              Cement Report

────────────────────────────────────────────
           South Azadegan Oilfield Project

               Wellhead diagram
               Hole Section Summary for EOWR

 3.3.2 17 ½” Hole Section, 13 3/8” Casing

      3.3.2.1  General
                               13-3/8” Casing @ ±2182m (5m TVD below top of Gurpi FM)Casing Point

      3.3.2.3  Sequence of Operation

    Make up 17-1/2” BHA as per BHA Design. RIH, tag top of cement, Drill out cement & shoe and rat hole cement.
      Work on junk sub.
    Watch carefully for any indication of abnormal torque, since there might be a possibility of shoe being backed off.
        Report problem immediately.
    Continue drilling 3m below shoe. Clean out the rat hole, circulate clean.
     Drill ahead formation down to 25m below 20” casing shoe with hi-vis pill. (Pump Hi-Vis pill Periodically)

Note:
If use stabilizer to drill out shoe track, make sure the RPM & WOB are in low as possible until the stabilizer is out from shoe
Then Cont. Drilling 17-1/2” hole section with same BHA.

    Circulate and clean hole, POOH to surface and P/U the same bit & S. Stab @ 20m according to BHA program, RIH
         to bottom.

     Drill ahead 17-1/2” hole section down to 750 m. CBU.
    Condition trip to 150m, run back and displace mud in hole by 74 pcf KCL/Polymer.
    Continue drilling to 850m or End of Bit Life, circ to clean hole, POOH to surface.
    P/U new PDC bit and Drill ahead 17-1/2” hole section down to 1750 m, 5m meanwhile perform condition trip each
       300 m. If observe Chert particle while formation sampling, Follow Contingency Plan#01
    Cont. Drill ahead 17-1/2” hole section down to casing point @ ±2182m, 5m TVD below top of Gurpi formation
       meanwhile perform condition trip each 300 m.

Note: In case of observe Cherty zone (Pabdeh formation), Stop Drilling, circulate to clean the hole, POOH to surface and
change PDC bit by Tri Cone Bit.

Note:
       For this well Single-shot every 100m and multi-shot survey every 300m to Control Well Deviation or take gyro shot
         (5-6 shots each 500-600m)
       Adjust mud weight according to program and as hole condition dictate.
       Tight holes, minor stucks and bit balling are hole problems which happen in offset wells.
      TD should be adjusted in such a way to have 1 meters of rat hole for probable fillings, before running casing.
       Choke manifold and choke line must be lined up for SOFT SHUT-IN through pit or burning line.
       Ensure that adequate fishing tools for this section are available.
       Ensure that enough amount and type of LCM are available and keep ready to mix and pump LCM Pills to cure
          losses.
    At section TD, circulate, clean hole. Perform wiper condition trip to shoe and observe well @ shoe. RIH (back to
      BTM, check for any drag, CBU), Check for any fill. CBU on bottom. Spot 100 bbl. hi-vis pill @ bottom. POOH to
         surface
     If observed any indication of tight hole, run back to TD. W&R to make sure that the hole condition is good prior to
        run Log.

    Perform pre-job safety meeting. R/U casing running equipment. Run casing 68#, N-80, BTC with F. shoe below first
          joint & F. collar as per casing program (function test of F. shoe & F. Collar should be checked on surface) while
         Circ. BTM’s up at shoe with low GPM.
    Rig pump efficiency should be check in trip-tank before cement job.
    Casing shoe depth should be adjusted in such a way to have 1 meters of rat hole and enough stick up (1.5 m) on rig
          floor.
    When casing is at casing setting depth circulate while preparing for cementing M/U & test cementing lines. Circulate
         for 120% of casing annulus volume.
    Cement casing as per program. Any minor adjustment regarding the corrected depth will be done in due time.
        Displace with mud. Monitor for pressures.
    Bump plug with 1500 psi (Max 80% Casing Burst) / 15 min as CIT (In case of not observing bumping during CMT
         job, perform CIT W/1500psi (Max 80% of 13-3/8” Casing Burst)/15min with consider mud weight after D/O CMT
      7m above F. Shoe also consider MW while perform CIT), Record and report the final pressure, Bleed off pressure,
        check for back flow, if any back flow observed, hold the pressure inside casing until the cement get hard.
    H/U 20-3/*4” BOP Hold on casing string weight. Drop casing hanger. Slack off 80% of casing string weight. Test
        hanger to 800-1000 psi pressure. (Open base flange side outlet valves while testing).
      , Rough cut L.J. & L/D same, N/D 20-3/4” BOP, Dress and bevel casing neck.
     Install Casing head spool 20 ¾”-3000*13 ⅝”-5000, Pack p-seals and pressure test p-seals and cavity with maximum
      80% of rated casing collapse pressure. (1800 psi)
    Ensure that the casing is drifted; cleaned and tallied priors to RIH.
     Fill casing by non-LCM fresh mud.

    Make up 12-1/4” BHA as per BHA design include Junk Sub. RIH, Tag top of cement. Drill out plugs, collar, drill out

          test with 1500 psi/15min) drill out cement and shoe track (while RIH pick up and rack back 500m 5"DP.)

    R/U Logging Equipment, Run GR/CCL/CBL/VDL Log, POOH To Surface. L/D Logging Equipment.
    RIH W/12-1/4” PDC Bit and BHA to BTM
    Displace hole with new mud ±76 pcf. Perform FIT (3-5m below shoe: EMW (550 psi) should be equal to max.
        planned mud weight for next hole section plus 2 pcf),
    Resume DRLG 12-1/4” Hole Section to 3915m (9-5/8” CSG point top of Fahliyan) while perform condition trip each
       300 m.

    Note: use single shot each 100m and multi shot each 300m to Control Well Deviation or take gyro shot (5-6 shots each
500-600m)

Note:
       Pre-plan for different loss control conditions including LCM materials in different sizes & cement plug.
       BOP's test every week. (every 2 weeks)
       Choke line and choke manifold must be lined up for Soft SHUT-IN.
       Adjust mud weight according to nearby wells and as hole condition dictation.
       The Section TD maybe changed based on formation tops detected by the wellsite geologist.
       The mud engineer shall check the mud properties as pre-planned time schedule.
       Ensure that all drilling tools and equipment are on well site in serviceable condition.
       Ensure that adequate fishing tools are available for this section.
       Ensure that enough amounts of LCM are available at location.
    At TD, circulate and clean the hole prior to condition trip. Observe the hole for one hour @ casing shoe. RIH to
        bottom and Pump 50 bbl hi-vis pill, circulate to clean hole (check bottoms up for any influx), Spot 70 bbl hi-vis Pill
         prior to POOH.
     It’s necessary to control drilling fluid rheology and especially solid content. It’s practically proven that excessive
         solid percent can reduce ROP significantly and cause major drilling difficulties. So, it’s necessary to check solid
         control equipment's and shaker screen inventory of the rig prior to drilling this hole section.
   POOH to surface. If observed any indication of drag, run back to bottom. Wash and ream to make sure that the hole
         condition is good prior run log.
    R/U wireline logging equipment. Run Wire line Loggin (Full Suite logs/ Deviation tool/MDT) as program. R/D
         wireline.
    RIH and condition hole prior to run casing and spot hi-vis pill.
    Change top rams to 9-5/8”.
    R/U casing running equipment. Perform pre-job safety meeting.
    Use Torque Monitoring System (TMS).
    Run casing as per casing program. Run 9-5/8” casing 53.5#, 110SS, Permium Gas tight Connection with F. shoe
       below first joint & F. collar, as per casing program (function of float shoe and float collor should be ckecked on
         surface)while Circ. BTM’s up at shoe with low GPM.

      3.3.4.2  Formation Sampling
NOTE: In addition to this general rule, geologist may decide to take more samples.
Well site geologist is fully responsible to determine the frequency of cutting sampling, and mud logging crew shall take
sample based on well site geologist’s advice. In deviated/Horizontal interval the sampling shouldn’t be limited to 20 m, and
the well site geologist should take care of sampling and collecting sample of any cuttings with different characteristics.

      3.3.4.3  Sequence of Operations
    Pick up 8-1/2” bit and bit sub and junk sub, RIH to Tag top of cement. Drill out plugs, collar, drill out F. collar and
        cement to 7m above shoe (In case of observe no bumping during cementing perform casing integrity test with 1500
        psi/15min) drill out cement and shoe track and shoe.
     Drilling 8 1/2" hole (3-5m) to 3918m.
    Work on junk sub, Circulate and clean hole, POOH to Surf. L/D 8-1/2” bit & junk sub.
    R/U logging equipment, Run GR/CCL/CBL/VDL/SBT, POOH & R/D logging Equip.
    P/U & M/U New 8 1/2" PDC bit (Optional performance) and S. Stab @ 20m, RIH to bottom.
    Circulate and clean hole, displace hole with new mud (100 pcf KCL Polymer Mud), perform FIT. (EMW (720 psi)
        should be equal to max. planned mud weight for 8-1/2” hole section plus 2 PCF),
     Drilling 8-1/2” vertical Hole Section to 4180m (7” Liner Point) while use single-shot every 100m and multi-shot
        survey every 300m)
    Perform Condition trip as POOH to 3915m, Observe the well, & run back to BTM, C.B.U, POOH to surface.
    R/U logging equipment, Run Full Suite logs/ Deviation tool/MDT, POOH & R/D logging Equip.
    R/U casing running equipment. Run and hang 7" liner from ±3815 mMD to ±4180mMD, (Hang 100 m above 9 5/8"
      CSG shoe, procedure of setting LNR will be issued in due time ).

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
           South Azadegan Oilfield Project

   Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 58 of 181

    M/U and torque up the shoe-to-shoe joint (32ppf, 110SS, gas tight premium connection. F/U first joint then P/U &
      M/U second joint of 7” liner, run & F/U same. Break circulation and function test shoe.
    P/U float Collar, clean threads, M/U and use baker lock for its box. M/U 1 joint 7” liner (32ppf, 110SS, gas tight
       premium connection) above float Collar & run & F/U same, break circulation and check float valve. P/U LDC, clean
         threads, M/U and use baker lock for its box, add one more 7” liner (32ppf, 110SS, gas tight premium connection),
        Break circulation and check float valve.
    Run 7” liner as per Tally, (Centralizer should be installed based on 7” LNR tally) F/U all 5 first joints, and then F/U
        every 5 joint. M/U liner hanger assembly as per job specialist instruction.
    Record pick-up and slack-off weight of liner.
    RIH 1 stand DP. Break & continue circulation as 1.2 cap of liner string. Check liner hanger for leaks.
    R/D liner running equipment
    Continue RIH on DP’s while drifting DP’s. F/U each 10 stands. With the liner assembly shoe at 9 5/8” casing shoe,
        break circulation and check free circulation through the liner assembly. Circulate at least 1.2 volume of Liner + Drill
        Pipe capacity.
    Continue RIH to liner setting depth, wash down last stand to bottom. Tag bottom. Continue circulation for at least
      120% of the liner and DP’s volume. Space out the string to have cement manifold at proper position.
    Circulation test at following flow rates: 20, 30, 40, 50 SPM, checking for losses, record results in IADC and advice
      DSV.
    Record pick-up and slack-off weight of liner at bottom.
    Drop ball and set liner hanger as per job specialist instruction. Test hanger setting in place. Shear ball catcher and
         establish circulation. Release setting tool from liner and make sure that it’s free, as per job specialist procedure.

Note: in case if it is not possible to set liner hanger, set liner shoe in TD then release liner hanger setting tools.

    Perform Pre-Job safety meeting with all personnel involved on the operation as well as the client representative. Read
        and follow the risk assessment for this job, Ensure communication between cement compant and rig floor
    Cement liner as per program. Perform liner integrity test w/ 3000 psi /15 min while cementing (max 80% of liner
         burst with consider mud weight)
    Pump Chemical wash.
    Pump spacer.
    Pump cement slurry.
    Release pump down plug.
    Pump 3 bbl cement slurry (as a part of displacement).
    Displace cement slurry with mud (decrease flow rate to 4 BPM for last 10 bbl displacement volume).
   POOH to 3350 m (depth will be corrected after finalize CMT program), flush string, Cont. POOH to Surface. L/D
         setting tools,

    Note: LNR running procedure will be issue in due time.

Note:
       Check the rig pump efficiency in order to prevent any kind of failure in the cementing job.
       Surge tank, cement unit, batch mixer and dry cement lines should be checked before the cementing job
       commencement in presence of well site supervisor, Tool pusher and cement supervisor to prevent any blockage
        during the cement job. (Get a confirmation from well site supervisor)
        Drill water, spacer, slurry solution and dry cement samples from each silo should be taken during the job and sent to
         lab for analyzing.
       Check the pit for spacer mixing to be clean, to prevent any contamination.
      Amount of cement and additives to be calculated based on the final cementing program.
       Double check the calculation with the well site supervisor (or well site engineer) before the job.

       Perform risk assessment and discuss contingency plan with the well site supervisor.

    P/U & M/U 8-1/2" Bit & new BHA & RIH to top of cement, D/O cement to L. Liner 7”, circulation and clean the

      3.3.4.4  Logging program:
Open hole: Full Suite logs/ Deviation tool/MDT
Cased hole: SBT, CAST or USIT

      3.3.4.5  Well Flowing
       The main objectives of the 96 hrs clean up and well flowing is to displace completion fluid, possible mud losses, and
        flow back the reservoir fluid until achieving the clean up operation criteria. The clean up operation procedure by rig
           is categorized as follows:
       Rigging up all related-equipment and perform hydro-test
       Retrieving barriers (plug, TWV) and landing protection sleeve in TRSSSV nipple
       Displacing the wellbore via CT with suitable method and compatible fluid
       Flowing the well over 96hrs thru different choke sizes while testing integrity and surface sampling
       Retrieving protection sleeve, inject 5 bbl diesel Close TRSSSV, Set BPV in tubing hanger, secure well & release rig.

Note: Detail Clean Up and Well Flowing Program Will Be Issued in Due Time.

      3.3.4.6  Contingency Plan
Contingency Plan #1
   POOH to Surface to change PDC Bit, P/U & M/U TCI Bit & RIH to Previous Depth, Perform Wiper Trip & Run
        back to BTM. Take survey as multi-shot, continue drilling to pass Cherty formation. Stand Back required & DPs,
     HWDPs and DC.

   Contingency Plan #2
    Perform Tandem scraper trip for dry test.
    Dry test with 2000psi differential press between water cushion and Fahliyan datum press. POOH to surface (inject
       Cement in L. Lap if required).

  5.2 Expected Challenges

 5.2.1 26” Section drom surface to 150 m

Challenges

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
           South Azadegan Oilfield Project

   Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 68 of 181

       Complete losses
       Hole cleaning issues due to large hole size
       Bit Blling

Recommendations

     Keep mud weight as low as possible to minimize formation losses.
     Optimize the procedure to fight the losses (see the guide at the end of this mud program) and be
       ready with LCM material to mix LCM pill as quick as possible, it is recommended to 8 ppb Mica
   M by 8 ppb Mica C to active system from start the Drilling up to 50 mt to prevent the probable
      Complete Losses.
     Keep Low GPM from Start drilling to 50 mt to prevent the Probable Complete Losses
     Adequate surface volume should be prepared in advance. A suitable quantitative of Drill water
      must be always available on location.
     Do not permit to use The Local Water (Hoor).
     Pump hi-vis pill at each connection to ensure good hole cleaning.
     Maintain adequate viscosity to clean the hole at anticipated penetration rates.
       All solids control equipment should be serviced and brought to optimum operating condition.

      Run all solids control equipment continuously while circulating.

       Utilize the finest mesh screens possible on flow line cleaners.

       Sand trap should be controlled and settled drilled solid should be dumped.

    A final high viscosity sweep should be pumped prior to pulling out of the hole to run casing.
      It is recommended that a high viscosity pill be spotted on bottom prior to running the casing.
       For Get sufficient pre-Hydrate gel its better after Mixing Gel left it on pit at least 6 hours before use.

      The Bentonite should be added slow enough to prevent balling and clogging. The initial yield
   depends in part on the quality of the surface equipment. Normally the slurry becomes thicker with
   time and agitation.

      The PH should be maintained around 10.5 - 11 with additions of Caustic Soda. This aids in
   hydrating the Bentonite, reducing corrosion rates, and minimizing the solubility of contaminants.

      In case of shortage in Drill Water XC‐Polymer HV sweep pill should be pumped just as each
   single is drilled, the pill should then be pumped around, allowing at least one lag time before stopping
  pumping for connection.

 5.2.2 17 ½” Section from 150 m to 2182 m
Challenges

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
           South Azadegan Oilfield Project

   Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 69 of 181

      Hole cleaning issue.
       Possible severe losses.
       Shale
      Marl
       Tight Hole and Hole stability

Recommendations

      It is mandatory to monitor closely the rheological properties in order to ensure adequate hole
       cleaning, maximizing the flow rates to assist in cuttings removal.
      Poor hole cleaning is indicated by an increase in pump pressure, torque and drag, over-pull at
       connections and by a lack of an appropriate amount of cuttings on the shakers relative to the ROP
      and hole size.
     Periodic high viscosity sweeps will help assess the hole cleaning efficiency.
     When Faced with marl lithology must be pump Low Vis pill every single to prevent increase
      Rheology unusually. Process of the cutting removing in the Shaker Screens by Mud Engineer
       could be the best issue to pump Hi vis pill or Low Vis and Adjust The GPM amount due to have a
      good hole cleaning.
     Mud can be treated with additional shale inhibitor if deemed necessary to control shale reactivity
      and washouts.
     All the precautions have to be considered in order to take immediate reactions for any risk of
        circulation losses: Optimize the procedure to fight the losses (see the guide at the end of this mud
      program) and be ready with LCM materials to mix LCM pill as quick as possible.

     This section will be drill up to ±700 mt by Gel mud then Will be displaced by KCL Pol mud

     For Get sufficient pre-Hydrate gel its better after Mixing Gel left it on pit at least 6 hours before
        use.
     Test and report key mud properties three times per day. The MBT & Hardness should be tested
      and reported 3 times per day.
     Adequate surface volume should be prepared in advance. A suitable quantity of water must be
       always available on location.
     All solids control equipment should be serviced and brought to optimum operating condition.

     Also the mud density has to be carefully monitored at 68 pcf at the first then for prevent the tight
       hole and hole stability increase the mud weight gradually to 77 pcf .
     Require to maximum effitiency of solid control equipment to control the mud weight

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
           South Azadegan Oilfield Project

   Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 70 of 181

                                The shakers should be run with the finest screens possible in
       accordance with the flow rate and minimize fluid losses from the shakers by checking deck angle
      and running appropriate screens at all times. Also  it should be monitored closely to ensure
        effective hole cleaning; if there is an inadequate or inconsistent supply of cuttings at the shaker, it
     may be necessary to increase the yield point further.

     The mud cleaner should be run with a 200 mesh screen, making sure that the cones are always
       clean and discharging heavy liquid. Check the working pressure in order to have the mud cleaner
      working at the maximum efficiency.
     Sand trap should be controlled and settled drilled solid should be dumped.

     Before tripping the shakers must be circulated clean at normal pump rate while reciprocating the
         drill pipes and moving the drill pipes up and down.
     Keep pH around the 9 - 9.5 while drilling the marl lithology to prevent the solving the marl in
     mud and cuases to dispersion and drop the PH.

        Measuring of the MBT is the direct indication of formation reactivity. MBT of range of < 12.5
        lb/bbl is acceptable. Above this range, mud Rheology will start to shoot up to uncontrollable
        levels. Using fine screens & high speed centrifuges will help in maintaining lower values of MBT
      by discarding the fine solids and consequently minimize the dilution rate.
       The Aghajari formation in Azadegan Field from surface to 1274 m consists of a repetition of
      brown to grey calcareous sandstones, chert debris and gypsum-veined red marl. A fauna of
       brackish to freshwater was observed, together with more marine fauna.

 5.2.3 12 ¼” Section from 2182 to 3915 m

Challenges

       Hole instability issues
       Wellbore losses.
       Shale
       Tight hole and well instability

Recommendations

      It is mandatory to monitor closely the rheological properties in order to ensure adequate hole
       cleaning, run Hydraulic calculation to ensure that mud carrying capacity is enough to meet this
         critical goal.
     Mud can be treated with additional shale inhibitor if deemed necessary to control shale reactivity
      and washouts.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
           South Azadegan Oilfield Project

   Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 71 of 181

     Optimize the procedure to fight the losses (see the guide at the end of this mud program) and be
       ready with adequate LCM material to mix LCM pill as quick as possible.

     The previouse mud in hole must be dump , due to have enoght surface volume , keep 300 bbl
      heavy mud in mud pits as kill mud.

     The concentration of KCl in the system is insufficient; shale cuttings will be soft and mushy. If
       the concentration of KCl is sufficient, they will retain their integrity. As drilling commences the
       potassium ion becomes depleted and must be replenished. The rate of depletion provides an
        indication of how reactive the shales are. While drilling gumbo, the appearance of soft mushy
        cuttings at surface is a good indication that the potassium concentration should be increased.
       Other indicators include a drilled solids increase, a CEC increase and the occurrence of tight hole
       or mud rings.

     Since this system is very solids-sensitive, it is best to mix the system from scratch instead of
       converting an existing drilling fluid (containing drill solids) to a potassium chloride system.

     This system is very sensitive to solids and hardness contamination so the first step in mixing this
       system is to treat the hardness.

     Due to necessary of directional drilling, it is recommended to pump (at the first 20 bbl Low Vis
         pill follow by 20 bbl Hi Vis Pill should be pumped every stand) for clean the hole. For better
       understanding of hole cleaning procedure based on hole angels please refer to technical appendix.
     Due to face with torque and drag , it is recommended to mix water base mud lubricant 1 – 3% vol
       reduce torque and drag while drilling horizontal or vertical

      In the directional drilling section it is recommended to use Glass beads 2 – 3 ppb as due to have
       torque and drag.

     The mud weight in this interval is critical for successful drilling, hole condition will dictate the
        density. Mud weight will be increased by adding CaCO3 F.

    Since this system is very solids-sensitive, it is best to mix the system from scratch instead of

Interval Discussion
Gel Mud system will be simply formulated with Bentonite to obtain the required suspending properties and form a good filter
cake. Makeup water should be checked and treated with SODA ASH to treat out hardness as needed. Adequate surface
volume should be prepared in advance, allowing maximum pre-hydration time.
All solids control equipment should be serviced and brought to optimum operating condition.
Run all solids control equipment continuously while circulating.
The mud density has to be carefully monitored and adapted to the well conditions, it is suggested to maintain the mud weight
at 65 - 67 pcf according to the well stability, this will be done by running the solids control equipment, control and dump
settled drilled solids from sand trap and diluting the active system with new mixed mud as required.
Due to the large hole size, the hole should be swept frequently with HV pills made by Gel Mud (Viscosity: 70 - 80 sec/lt).
Losses may occur while drilling this top section specially between surface up to 50 mt which for prevent this matter it is
recommended to add 4 ppb Mica M and 4 PPB Mica C together. Mud should be treated with SODIUM BICARBONATE
before cement job to prevent cement contamination.
It is recommended that a high viscosity pill be spotted on bottom prior to running the casing.

 5.3.2 17 ½” Section from 150 to 2182 m

  The 17 ½" section will be drilled from 20" Casing Shoe @ ±150 m to ±850 mt with previous mud in hole (Gel mud) then
   at depth will be displace by KCL/Polymer mud and drill to an estimated section TD 2182 m.
  At this system uses Salt/KCl as the source of density and Clay inhibition in the system. Mud weight will be Increase to 87
   pcf by Barite as weighting agent; Rheology will be controlled by the use of Xanthan gum. Also Fluid loss will be keep
   under control by the addition of polymeric chemicals such as, Starch LV. Addition of Drilling Detergent (As contingency)
   and KCl (3 - 5 % wt.) to the mud system can prevent bit balling and consequently can help to optimize the drilling
   operation while drilling sticky Marl and Clay section. Fluid loss should be kept below 10 cc, meanwhile.
   Also pH should be maintained within 9 - 9.5 while drilling Marl and keep within 9.5 – 10 while drilling Anhydrite.
  At this section for the inhibition of system must be use PHPA (0.25 ppb) which adheres to the surface of the drilled
   cuttings, encapsulating them, and also coating the well bore reducing the hydration of clays in the formation.

────────────────────────────────────────────
           South Azadegan Oilfield Project

   Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 76 of 181

               17 1/2" HOLE SIZE                        13 3/8" Casing Size
           FROM : 150 MT                                         TO : 2182 MT
          2032 meter to drill in Aghajari & Gachsaran, Asmari, Pabdeh by KCl/Polymer mud
           DRILLING FLUID SYSTEM            Salt/KCl/PHPA/Polymer Mud

                                                                       Centrifuge

Interval Mud Discussion
   After drilling shoe track and rate hole with carried Bentonite mud from previous section Salt/KCl/Polymer mud will be
   employed to drilling this hole section.
   Saturated Salt Muds are primarily used to prevent excessive hole enlargement while drilling massive salt beds. They can
    also be used to reduce dispersion and hydration of shales and clays.
   Potassium chloride is the primary source of the potassium ions in this system. In case of soft, sticky red Marl & Clay
   which encountered problems such as bit balling additions of drilling KCl (3 - 5 %) to inhibit clays in Aghajari is
   recommended.

   Potassium is used in areas where inhibition is required to limit chemical alteration of shales. Potassium performance is
   based on cationic exchange of potassium for sodium or calcium ions on smectites and interlayered clays. Potassium-based
   muds perform best on shales containing large quantities of smectite or interlayered clays in the total clay fraction. Shallow
    shales, containing large amounts of montmorillonite.

   Potassium interactions with clay surfaces can be traced to two effects: ionic size and hydration energy. Potassium ions are
   of the proper size to fit snugly into the spaces between the two silica tetrahedral layers which contact each other in the
   formation of a three-layer clay packet. The ionic diameter of potassium is 2.66 Å, very close to the available distance of
   2.8 Å in the lattice space of the clay structure. A cation slightly smaller than 2.8 Å is desirable to allow for crystalline
   compaction.

   In addition of using KCl, despite Aghajari is an un-permeable formation and seems that isn’t necessary to control Fluid
    loss, Starch and PAC LV may be used to keep FL under control.

   While drilling this section Xanthan gum will be used as base of rheology to extend hole cleaning and providing suitable
  YP and LSRV. An YP between 15-20 lb/100ft2 for sufficient carrying capacity and a LSRYP at range of 7-15 to
   preventing any Barite sag in system is required.
   As it is seen in geological forecast, soft & sticky marls referred as Gumbo, contained large amount of Smectite and/or Illite
   could be associated with problems such as bit balling, mud rings, plugged flow lines and hole washout& cause low process
   of drilling and unnecessary trips and some more difficulties to drill the section as fast as possible. With all those problems
   reduction of ROP was inevitable, blinding of shaker screens, excessive over pull on trips, washout and stuck pipes has
   been experienced as well. For problem Solve can add 3-5 % KCl in system.
   In case of necessary the inhibition of system can be increased by adding PHPA which adheres to the surface of the drilled
    cuttings, encapsulating them, and also coating the well bore reducing the hydration of clays in the formation.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
           South Azadegan Oilfield Project

   Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 77 of 181

   Also while drilling this section encounter with Ca2+ based Gypsum is inventible. Gyp is calcium sulfate with water of
    crystallization (CaSO4.2H2O), Calcium sulfate contamination is similar to cement contamination because both liberate
   calcium ions, which in turn cause flocculation. Unlike cement, calcium sulfate does not because a PH increase since it
   supplies a sulfate ion in lieu of a hydroxyl ion. The sulfate ion contributes to flocculation of clay solids, although its effect
    is small compared to calcium ions. The severity of flocculation depends to a great degree on clay content. When CaSO4
   causes the calcium concentration to increase and viscosity may fluctuate drastically. As calcium ion concentrations further
    increase, a base exchange occurs in which sodium montmorillonite becomes calcium montmorillonite. There are several
   methods for handling CaSO4 contamination. The drilling fluid can be maintained as a low-calcium fluid by chemically
    precipitating by adding soda ash.

                                                Salt / KCL / Polymer mud

          Product N.                       Ppb                   Product Function

                               MIXING PROCEDURE

             1. PH , chloride , calcium and total hardness of drill water ( that water which will be used for mud
          making ) must be check by Mud engineer

             2. Fill the mud tank with Drill water
             3. add KCL as inhibitor
             4. add Salt to reach mentioned mud weight
             5. Remove Hardness by add Soda ash
             6. adjust PH & alkalinity by Caustic soda and Lime
             7. add Starch LV to control fluid loss
             8. add Xanthan gum to reach good rheology
             9. Add Barite powder to reach Mentioned Weight
           10.Check the pH and keep in range by add caustic soda
            11. Mix Biocide as need

Interval Mud Discussion

The water-based KCL Polymer system provides effective inhibition when drilling reactive shale’s through below mechanism:

     Potassium Chloride provides a source of Potassium ions, which are small enough to fit between the clay platelets without
      distorting the shale lattice. The Potassium ions are adsorbed on the exchangeable caution sites in the shale lattice and this

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
          South Azadegan Oilfield Project

Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 82 of 181

 holds  the  platelets  together,  which  helps  to  minimize  clay  and  shale  hydration,  swelling  and  dispersion.
 A low solids, non-dispersed Salt/KCl/CaCo₃ Polymer drilling ﬂuid is selected for drilling this section. This system is
 used primarily to drill moderately reactive shale’s.  It can be used effectively at temperatures up to 275˚ F – 300˚

  If made a decision to run the Glycol System (KCL/Glycol/Polymer mud):

  The primary shale inhibition mechanisms in the system are:

 1. The cation exchange of potassium ions with the clay to reduce hydration and swelling in conjunction with;

 2. A partially hydrolyzed polyacrylamide (PHPA) which adheres to the surface of the drilled cuttings, encapsulating
 them, and also coating the well bore reducing the hydration of clays in the formation.

 3. Clouding out of Glycol: The above methods of shale inhibition from the KCl/PHPA system are enhanced by the
 clouding effect of the glycol which prevents pore pressure transmission. The stabilization mechanisms combine
 synergistically to provide inhibition close to that of non-aqueous ﬂuids.

 At a speciﬁc temperature (cloud point) a water soluble glycol starts to change from soluble to insoluble, forming a
 thermally activated emulsion (TAME). As the temperature increases the glycol starts to come out of solution and the
 liquid begins clouding. Eventually the single phase liquid separates into two separate phases.

 A: Glycol and water together – at ambient temperature the glycol is 100% water soluble

 B: Solution is heated until the glycol reaches ‘cloud point’ and becomes insoluble.

 Cloud point is a function of the following variables:

   Salinity of the solution
   Molecular weight and type
   Type of electrolyte
   Concentration of the glycol

 Research has concluded that the glycol is associated by hydrogen bonding to the reactive sites of clays. During this
 adsorption process, water is displaced and ordered structures of glycols are formed. There is little depletion of the glycol
 from the ﬂuid when drilling, the glycol returning to solution as the cutting moves up the wellbore and the ﬂuid
 temperature drops below the cloud point. This supports the theory that the bonding between the clay and the glycol is
 transitory, and there is some mechanism for association and disassociation due to changes in the environment.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
           South Azadegan Oilfield Project

   Contract No:          F – 20 Well Drilling Program                  Page 83 of 181

       It can be concluded that the shale inhibition and formation protection will be achieved by one or both of the following
     mechanisms:

            The glycol displaces water from adsorption sites on the clay minerals present in shales, especially in the
             presence of potassium Ions.
            Blocking the formation pores, preventing further ingress of invasive ﬂuids by clouding out preventing pore
              pressure transmission.

     During drilling operations, shale cuttings should be monitored continuously for inhibition. If the concentration of
      inhibitors in the system is insufficient, shale cuttings will be soft and mushy. If the concentration of inhibitors is
      sufficient, they will retain their integrity. In addition of this controlling API filter press is a key parameter to deal with.

    Due to Shale layer in Pabdeh formation different additives such as PHPA and Glycol should be added to active system
    any prevent any hole problem.
    The mud will be formulated and treated to in inhibit and encapsulate the reactive shale found in this area. The PHPA
     content should be run at 0.25-1 ppb throughout the section. PHPA will be added to the mud on the rig prior to drilling
     ahead. The success of this system is to achieve and maintain the required concentration of KCL – PHPA and GLYCOL
      in the system as early as possible in the drilling operation. The main function for the KCL is the primary source of the
      inhibitive K+ cation the inhibitive property of K+ while drilling reactive shale and clay stone is commonly known and
     understood. The K+ level derived from the KCL is determined from either historical date or shale characterization and is
     usually between 3% to 5% KCl in this specific case we would recommend a concentration of ± 11-18 ppb.
